LIÇÃO 12 · UNIDADE A1
Possessivos e demonstrativos
Possessives and demonstratives
After this lesson you will be able to say whose something is (o meu livro, a nossa casa),
point at things near you, near the listener and far from both (este, esse, aquele), use the
neutral forms isto, isso, aquilo for things you cannot name, and make the contractions
these words form with de, em and a.
1. Os possessivos
A possessive in Portuguese agrees with the thing owned, not with the owner. O meu carro
is "my car" whether the speaker is a man or a woman: carro is masculine, so the possessive
is masculine.
Owner masc. sing. fem. sing. masc. pl. fem. pl. English
eu o meu a minha os meus as minhas my
tu o teu a tua os teus as tuas your (informal)
ele / ela / você o seu a sua os seus as suas his, her, your (formal)
nós o nosso a nossa os nossos as nossas our
vocês o vosso a vossa os vossos as vossas your (plural)
eles / elas o seu a sua os seus as suas their
O meu irmão e a minha irmã moram no Porto.
My brother and my sister live in Porto.
Onde estão as tuas chaves?
Where are your keys?
A nossa casa é pequena, mas o nosso jardim é grande.
Our house is small, but our garden is big.
Meninos, os vossos livros estão na mesa.
Children, your books are on the table.
In European Portuguese the article normally goes in front of the possessive: o meu, not 
meu alone. The article is dropped only with close family in direct address and in a few fixed
phrases.
Minha filha, vem cá!
My daughter, come here!
POSSESSIVOS E DEMONSTRATIVOS
101

===== PAGE 102 =====

ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
Seu / sua is ambiguous: it can mean his, her, their or your (formal). To be clear,
Portuguese speakers prefer dele (his), dela (her), deles / delas (their) placed after
the noun: o carro dele (his car), a casa delas (their house). These forms never take
an article of their own and never change for the thing owned.
Esta é a mãe dele e aquele é o pai dela.
This is his mother and that is her father.
2. Os demonstrativos: este, esse, aquele
Portuguese has three degrees of distance, not two as in English.
Forma Distance English
este / esta / estes / estas near me, the speaker this / these (here)
esse / essa / esses / essas near you, the listener that / those (there by you)
aquele / aquela / aqueles / aquelas far from both of us that / those (over there)
Matching adverbs of place go with them: aqui (here), aí (there, by you), ali / lá (over
there).
Este livro aqui é meu.
This book here is mine.
Essa caneta aí escreve bem?
Does that pen there (by you) write well?
Aquele edifício ali é a câmara municipal.
That building over there is the town hall.
Estas laranjas são do Algarve, aquelas são de Espanha.
These oranges are from the Algarve, those ones are from Spain.
3. As formas neutras: isto, isso, aquilo
Use these when you are not naming a noun — an unknown object, an idea, a whole
situation. They never change for gender or number.
Neutro Distance English
isto near me this (thing, idea)
isso near you that
aquilo far from both that over there
O que é isto?
What is this?
POSSESSIVOS E DEMONSTRATIVOS
102

===== PAGE 103 =====

Isso não é verdade.
That is not true.
Aquilo ali é um castelo.
That thing over there is a castle.
DICA · TIP
If you can put a noun after the word, use este/esse/aquele. If you cannot, use isto/
isso/aquilo. Compare Este é bom (this one — a named thing) and Isto é bom (this
— whatever it is).
4. Contrações com os demonstrativos
Just like the articles, the demonstratives melt into de, em and (only for the aquele group) a.
+ este + esse + aquele Preposição
deste, desta, destes, destas desse, dessa, desses, dessas daquele, daquela, daqueles, daquelas de
neste, nesta, nestes, nestas nesse, nessa, nesses, nessas naquele, naquela, naqueles, naquelas em
a este, a esta... a esse, a essa... àquele, àquela, àqueles, àquelas a
The neutral forms behave the same way:
Preposição + isto + isso + aquilo
de disto disso daquilo
em nisto nisso naquilo
a a isto a isso àquilo
Gosto muito deste bairro.
I like this neighbourhood a lot.
Ela mora naquele prédio amarelo.
She lives in that yellow building.
Não sei nada disso.
I know nothing about that.
Nesta rua há três cafés.
On this street there are three cafés.
ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
De este, em aquele and a aquilo do not exist. The contraction is obligatory: deste, 
naquele, àquilo. Remember the accent on à-forms: àquele, àquilo.
POSSESSIVOS E DEMONSTRATIVOS
103

===== PAGE 104 =====

NOTA CULTURAL
On the phone, Portuguese speakers say Estou? or Está lá? to mean "Hello?". The
distance words carry over to time too: esta semana is the current week, essa
semana the one you just mentioned, and naquele tempo means "back in those days".

LIÇÃO 13 · UNIDADE A1
A rotina diária
The daily routine
After this lesson you will be able to describe your day from morning to night: getting up,
washing, getting dressed, going to work and going to bed. You will learn reflexive verbs
(verbs where the action comes back to the person doing it), where to put the little reflexive
pronoun in a Portuguese sentence, adverbs that say how often you do something, and the
words for the parts of the day.
1. Reflexive verbs — what they are
A reflexive verb describes an action that the subject does to itself. In English we often say
nothing at all ("I get up"), or we say "myself". In Portuguese the little word is obligatory.
In the dictionary these verbs end in -se: levantar-se (to get up), deitar-se (to go to bed), 
chamar-se (to be called). The -se is the pronoun for ele/ela/você; you change it to match
the subject.
Pronome Pronome reflexo English
eu me myself
tu te yourself (informal)
ele/ela/você se himself / herself / yourself (polite)
nós nos ourselves
eles/elas/vocês se themselves / yourselves
Here is the full present tense of levantar-se, a regular -ar verb. Notice that the verb ending
is completely normal; only the pronoun is new.
Pronome levantar-se English
eu levanto-me I get up
tu levantas-te you get up
ele/ela/você levanta-se he/she gets up, you get up
nós levantamo-nos we get up
eles/elas/vocês levantam-se they/you get up
A ROTINA DIÁRIA
107

===== PAGE 108 =====

ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
In the nós form the verb loses its final -s before -nos: levantamo-nos, not
"levantamos-nos". This happens with every reflexive verb: deitamo-nos, vestimo-
nos, sentamo-nos.
Eu levanto-me às sete da manhã.
I get up at seven in the morning.
A Ana deita-se muito tarde.
Ana goes to bed very late.
Nós vestimo-nos depois do pequeno-almoço.
We get dressed after breakfast.
Como é que tu te chamas?
What is your name? (literally: how do you call yourself?)
2. Two more reflexive models: -er and -ir
The pronouns never change. Only the verb endings follow the normal -er or -ir pattern you
already know.
Pronome esquecer-se (to forget) vestir-se (to get dressed) English
eu esqueço-me visto-me I forget / I get dressed
tu esqueces-te vestes-te you forget / you get dressed
ele/ela/você esquece-se veste-se he forgets / he gets dressed
nós esquecemo-nos vestimo-nos we forget / we get dressed
eles/elas/vocês esquecem-se vestem-se they forget / they get dressed
Vestir has a small vowel change in the eu form: e becomes i (visto-me). The verb despir-
se (to get undressed) does the same: dispo-me.
Eu visto-me sempre de preto.
I always dress in black.
Ela esquece-se das chaves todos os dias.
She forgets her keys every day.
Os meninos divertem-se no parque.
The children enjoy themselves in the park.
3. Where does the pronoun go?
This is the part that surprises learners. In European Portuguese the normal place for the
pronoun is after the verb, joined with a hyphen. This is called ênclise.
A ROTINA DIÁRIA
108

===== PAGE 109 =====

Ele lava-se de manhã.
He washes in the morning.
But certain words pull the pronoun in front of the verb (próclise). Learn them as a small list:
Trigger Example English
não, nunca, nada, ninguém (negatives) Eu não me levanto cedo. I don't get up early.
que, porque, quando, se (linking words) Ele diz que se deita tarde. He says that he goes to bed late.
quem, onde, como, o que (question words) Como é que te chamas? What's your name?
já, ainda, também, só, sempre, talvez Eu já me vesti. I've already got dressed.
todos, alguém, muitos (some quantity words) Todos se sentam à mesa. Everyone sits down at the table.
Nós não nos deitamos antes da meia-noite.
We don't go to bed before midnight.
Quando é que vocês se levantam?
When do you (plural) get up?
Ela nunca se esquece do meu aniversário.
She never forgets my birthday.
DICA · TIP
With ir + infinitivo (Lição 10) you have two correct options: Vou levantar-me cedo
or Vou-me levantar cedo. The first one, with the pronoun on the infinitive, is the
safer choice and always sounds natural in Portugal.
ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
Brazilian Portuguese normally puts the pronoun first ("Me levanto às sete"). In
Portugal a sentence never begins with the pronoun. Say Levanto-me às sete.
4. Adverbs of frequency and parts of the day
These words say how often. They usually go after the verb, or at the beginning of the
sentence.
A ROTINA DIÁRIA
109

===== PAGE 110 =====

Português English Exemplo
sempre always Tomo sempre café.
quase sempre almost always Ele chega quase sempre tarde.
muitas vezes often Vou muitas vezes ao ginásio.
às vezes sometimes Às vezes almoço fora.
raramente rarely Raramente vejo televisão.
nunca never Nunca me deito cedo.
todos os dias every day Trabalho todos os dias.
uma vez por semana once a week Nado uma vez por semana.
For the times of day, use de for a general period and da after a clock time:
Português English
de manhã in the morning
de tarde / à tarde in the afternoon
de noite / à noite at night
ao meio-dia at midday
à meia-noite at midnight
às oito da manhã at eight in the morning
às três da tarde at three in the afternoon
às dez da noite at ten at night
Levanto-me às seis da manhã e deito-me às onze da noite.
I get up at six in the morning and go to bed at eleven at night.
De tarde, ela estuda na biblioteca.
In the afternoon she studies in the library.
Ele nunca toma banho de manhã.
He never showers in the morning.
NOTA CULTURAL
In Portugal o pequeno-almoço (breakfast) is small: a coffee and a toast at the café
counter. O almoço (lunch) is usually between 12h30 and 14h00 and is the main meal. 
O jantar (dinner) is late, often after 20h00. Many people also have um lanche, a
snack around 17h00.

Acordar and levantar-se are not the same. Acordar means to wake up (open your
eyes); levantar-se means to get out of bed. Acordo às sete, mas só me levanto às sete
e meia.

A Marta acorda todos os dias às seis e meia, mas só se levanta às sete menos um

LIÇÃO 14 · UNIDADE A1
Comida e compras
Food and shopping
After this lesson you will be able to do your food shopping and eat out in Portugal: name the
food you want, ask for a quantity, understand a menu, order in a café or restaurant, ask the
price and pay. You will also learn the polite forms Portuguese people actually use — queria, 
pode dar-me, era só — instead of the blunt "eu quero".
1. Quantities — how much, how many
When you buy food you rarely say just the noun. You say a quantity, then de, then the food.
The de does not combine with an article here — you say um quilo de arroz, not "um quilo do
arroz".
Português English Exemplo
um quilo de… a kilo of… um quilo de batatas
meio quilo de… half a kilo of… meio quilo de tomates
duzentos gramas de… 200 grams of… duzentos gramas de queijo
um litro de… a litre of… um litro de leite
uma garrafa de… a bottle of… uma garrafa de vinho tinto
um pacote de… a packet of… um pacote de bolachas
uma lata de… a tin of… uma lata de atum
uma caixa de… a box of… uma caixa de ovos
uma fatia de… a slice of… uma fatia de fiambre
uma dúzia de… a dozen… uma dúzia de ovos
um bocado de… a bit of… um bocado de pão
Queria um quilo de maçãs, se faz favor.
I'd like a kilo of apples, please.
Dê-me meio quilo de queijo da ilha.
Give me half a kilo of island cheese.
Quanto custa uma garrafa de azeite?
How much does a bottle of olive oil cost?
COMIDA E COMPRAS
115

===== PAGE 116 =====

DICA · TIP
O grama is masculine, so the correct form is duzentos gramas, cem gramas. At the
counter people often shorten it and just say the number: Queria duzentos de presunto.
For 500 g everyone says meio quilo.
2. Polite requests — how to ask for things
"Eu quero um café" is grammatically correct but sounds rude, like a demand. Portuguese
uses softer formulas. Learn these five as fixed blocks; you do not need any new grammar to
use them.
Fórmula English Uso
Queria… I'd like… the standard polite request
Era só… Just… (that's all) very common in cafés
Pode dar-me…? Can you give me…? asking someone to hand you something
Pode trazer-me…? Can you bring me…? in a restaurant
Se faz favor / Por favor Please add it at the end
Queria is a past-tense-looking form of querer, but for now simply treat it as one word
meaning "I would like". It never changes when you talk about yourself.
Queria um galão e uma torrada, se faz favor.
I'd like a milky coffee and a toast, please.
Pode dar-me a ementa, por favor?
Can you give me the menu, please?
Era só isso, obrigado.
That's all, thank you.
Pode trazer-me mais pão, se faz favor?
Could you bring me some more bread, please?
ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
Do not translate "Can I have…?" word for word as "Posso ter…?" — that means "am I
allowed to own it?". Use Queria… or Pode dar-me…?
3. At the café and at the restaurant
Here is a short dialogue with the words in the order you will really hear them.
— Boa tarde. O que é que deseja?
— Good afternoon. What would you like?
COMIDA E COMPRAS
116

===== PAGE 117 =====

— Queria uma sopa e o bacalhau à Brás.
— I'd like a soup and the bacalhau à Brás.
— E para beber?
— And to drink?
— Uma água sem gás, se faz favor.
— A still water, please.
— A conta, se faz favor.
— The bill, please.
Português English
a ementa / a carta the menu
a entrada the starter
o prato principal the main course
a sobremesa the dessert
o prato do dia the dish of the day
a dose / a meia-dose the portion / the half portion
a conta the bill
o empregado / a empregada the waiter / the waitress
Quanto é? How much is it?
Posso pagar com cartão? Can I pay by card?
Está incluído? Is it included?
NOTA CULTURAL
In a Portuguese restaurant the bread, cheese and olives that arrive before you order
are os couvertes — they are not free. If you do not want them, say politely Pode levar,
se faz favor. A meia-dose is a genuine half portion at about 60% of the price and is
completely normal to order.
DICA · TIP
Coffee vocabulary in Portugal: um café or uma bica = an espresso; um abatanado
= a longer black coffee; uma meia de leite = half coffee half milk in a cup; um galão
= the same in a tall glass; um garoto = an espresso with a dash of milk.
4. Asking the price and saying what you like
Two verbs do most of the work: custar (to cost) and gostar de (to like). Both are regular -
ar verbs.
COMIDA E COMPRAS
117

===== PAGE 118 =====

Pronome custar English
eu custo I cost
tu custas you cost
ele/ela/você custa it costs
nós custamos we cost
eles/elas/vocês custam they cost
In practice you only need two forms: Quanto custa…? for one thing and Quanto
custam…? for several.
Quanto custa o pão?
How much is the bread?
Quanto custam os ovos?
How much are the eggs?
São três euros e cinquenta.
That's three euros fifty.
Gostar always takes de, and de contracts with the article: de + o = do, de + a = da, de +
os = dos, de + as = das (Lição 09).
Eu gosto de peixe, mas não gosto de marisco.
I like fish, but I don't like seafood.
Ela gosta do vinho verde.
She likes the vinho verde.
Nós não gostamos das sardinhas assadas.
We don't like grilled sardines.
ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
Never drop the de. "Gosto café" is wrong; say Gosto de café. And the person who
likes is the subject: Eu gosto de chocolate — not the Spanish-style "me gusta"
pattern.

a manteiga the butter

DICA · TIP
Two false friends at the table: a sobremesa is the dessert, not a "table setting"; and o
pequeno-almoço is breakfast, while a entrada is the starter, not the entrance to the
meal in the American sense (in the USA "entrée" means the main course — in Portugal
it does not).

LIÇÃO 15 · UNIDADE A1
Estar a + infinitivo e o tempo
The present continuous and the weather
After this lesson you will be able to say what is happening right now using the European
Portuguese construction estar a + infinitivo, talk about the weather and the seasons, and
describe a scene as you see it. This is the last lesson of the A1 unit, so it also brings
together the present tense, prepositions and adjectives you already know.
1. Estar a + infinitivo — what is happening now
English says "I am eating". Portugal says estou a comer: the verb estar conjugated, then
the little word a, then the infinitive of the main verb (the dictionary form ending in -ar, -er or
-ir).
The formula never changes:
estar (conjugated) + a + infinitivo
Pronome estar + a + infinitivo English
eu estou estou a comer I am eating
tu estás estás a comer you are eating
ele/ela/você está está a comer he/she is eating, you are eating
nós estamos estamos a comer we are eating
eles/elas/vocês estão estão a comer they are eating, you are eating
Only estar changes. The second verb stays in the infinitive for ever: a falar, a beber, a
dormir.
Eu estou a estudar português.
I am studying Portuguese.
O que é que tu estás a fazer?
What are you doing?
Eles estão a ver televisão na sala.
They are watching television in the living room.
Nós estamos a esperar o autocarro.
We are waiting for the bus.
ESTAR A + INFINITIVO E O TEMPO
122

===== PAGE 123 =====

ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
Brazilian Portuguese uses the gerund: "estou comendo". In Portugal that form is not
used in everyday speech. Always say estou a comer. Never write both: "estou a
comendo" is wrong.
ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
Do not forget the little a. "Estou comer" is wrong. And the second verb is never
conjugated: not "estou a como", but estou a comer.
2. When to use it — and when NOT to
Portuguese uses the simple present much more than English does. Use estar a + infinitivo
only for an action in progress at this very moment.
Situation Português English
happening right now Ela está a dormir. She is sleeping (now).
a habit, a routine Ela dorme oito horas por noite. She sleeps eight hours a night.
a general truth O sol nasce a leste. The sun rises in the east.
a future plan Amanhã vou a Lisboa. Tomorrow I'm going to Lisbon.
Normalmente trabalho em casa, mas hoje estou a trabalhar no escritório.
I normally work at home, but today I'm working in the office.
Ele fala três línguas, mas neste momento está a falar inglês.
He speaks three languages, but right now he's speaking English.
Não posso atender, estou a conduzir.
I can't answer, I'm driving.
DICA · TIP
Useful time words that go with this structure: agora (now), neste momento (at this
moment), hoje (today), ainda (still), já (already). Ainda estou a comer. = I'm still
eating.
3. Two close relatives: acabar de and começar a
Two more useful patterns built the same way, with a preposition plus an infinitive.
ESTAR A + INFINITIVO E O TEMPO
123

===== PAGE 124 =====

Estrutura English Exemplo
acabar de + infinitivo to have just done something Acabo de chegar. (I've just arrived.)
começar a + infinitivo to start doing something Começo a trabalhar às nove.
continuar a + infinitivo to keep on doing something Continua a chover.
deixar de + infinitivo to stop doing something Ele deixa de fumar. (He gives up smoking.)
Ela acaba de sair de casa.
She has just left the house.
Nós começamos a trabalhar às oito e meia.
We start work at half past eight.
Continua a chover desde manhã.
It keeps raining since this morning.
4. O tempo — the weather
Careful: o tempo means both "the weather" and "the time". Context tells you which.
Most weather expressions use one of four verbs: estar, fazer, haver or the special one-form
verbs chover and nevar.
Verbo Português English
estar Está bom tempo. The weather is good.
estar Está mau tempo. The weather is bad.
estar Está sol. / Está nublado. It's sunny. / It's cloudy.
estar Está calor. / Está frio. It's hot. / It's cold.
fazer Faz calor. / Faz frio. It's hot. / It's cold.
fazer Faz vento. It's windy.
haver Há nevoeiro. There's fog.
haver Há trovoada. There's a thunderstorm.
chover Chove. / Está a chover. It rains. / It's raining.
nevar Neva. / Está a nevar. It snows. / It's snowing.
Chover and nevar only ever appear in the ele form — there is no "I rain". To ask about the
weather, say:
Como está o tempo hoje?
What's the weather like today?
Está a chover e faz muito vento.
It's raining and it's very windy.
ESTAR A + INFINITIVO E O TEMPO
124

===== PAGE 125 =====

No verão faz muito calor no Alentejo.
In summer it's very hot in the Alentejo.
Hoje estão vinte e dois graus.
It's twenty-two degrees today.
ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
English says "I am hot" and "it is hot" with the same verb. Portuguese does not. For
the weather: Está calor / Faz calor. For a person: Tenho calor (literally "I have
heat"). "Estou quente" means your body is physically hot, and can sound odd.
5. As estações do ano — the seasons
Português English Quando?
a primavera spring março, abril, maio
o verão summer junho, julho, agosto
o outono autumn setembro, outubro, novembro
o inverno winter dezembro, janeiro, fevereiro
Use em or the contraction no/na with the seasons: na primavera, no verão, no outono, 
no inverno.
No inverno chove muito no Porto.
In winter it rains a lot in Porto.
Na primavera está tempo ameno.
In spring the weather is mild.
Gosto do outono porque não faz calor nem frio.
I like autumn because it's neither hot nor cold.
NOTA CULTURAL
Portugal has very different weather from north to south. In the north (Porto, Braga) it
rains often, even in summer. In the Algarve and the Alentejo summers are dry and can
pass 40 graus. In Lisbon a cold, windy, rainy winter day is called um dia de cão, and
people say Está um frio de rachar — it's freezing cold.
Vocabulário
o tempo the weather; the time
o sol the sun
a chuva the rain
a neve the snow
o vento the wind
a nuvem the cloud
ESTAR A + INFINITIVO E O TEMPO

A estação has two meanings: a season of the year, and a station (a estação de

LIÇÃO 16 · UNIDADE A2
Pretérito Perfeito Simples — verbos regulares
The simple past tense — regular verbs
Welcome to the A2 unit. Until now you have only talked about the present. In this lesson you
learn your first past tense: the Pretérito Perfeito Simples, the tense Portuguese uses for
finished actions — what you did yesterday, last week, last year. You will learn the full
endings for -ar, -er and -ir verbs, the small spelling changes that keep the sound right
(fiquei, comecei, cheguei), and the time expressions that go with the tense.
1. What this tense is for
The Pretérito Perfeito Simples (we will call it the PPS) describes an action that started and
finished in the past. The action is over. Think of it as a closed box.
Ontem eu trabalhei até às sete.
Yesterday I worked until seven.
Nós comemos num restaurante muito bom.
We ate in a very good restaurant.
Ela partiu na semana passada.
She left last week.
One Portuguese form covers two English forms. Eu falei can be "I spoke" or "I have spoken"
— Portuguese does not separate them the way English does.
DICA · TIP
Do not translate the English present perfect ("I have eaten") with the Portuguese 
tenho comido. That Portuguese form means something different — a repeated action
over recent time. For a single finished action always use the PPS: comi.
2. -AR verbs: falar
Take the infinitive, remove -ar, and add the endings. The stem of falar is fal-.
Pronome falar English
eu falei I spoke / I have spoken
tu falaste you spoke
ele/ela/você falou he/she spoke, you spoke
nós falámos we spoke
eles/elas/vocês falaram they spoke, you spoke
PRETÉRITO PERFEITO SIMPLES — VERBOS REGULARES
130

===== PAGE 131 =====

The endings on their own: -ei, -aste, -ou, -ámos, -aram.
Eu estudei português durante dois anos.
I studied Portuguese for two years.
Tu compraste o pão?
Did you buy the bread?
A Ana trabalhou em Lisboa.
Ana worked in Lisbon.
Nós jantámos às nove.
We had dinner at nine.
Eles visitaram o museu.
They visited the museum.
ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
The nós form is the trap. Present: falamos (we speak). Past: falámos (we spoke).
Only the accent tells them apart, and in Portugal the accent is written and pronounced
— the past has a clear open "a". Brazilian spelling writes both without the accent; in
European Portuguese you must write falámos, trabalhámos, comprámos.
3. -ER verbs: comer
Remove -er. The stem of comer is com-.
Pronome comer English
eu comi I ate
tu comeste you ate
ele/ela/você comeu he/she ate, you ate
nós comemos we ate
eles/elas/vocês comeram they ate, you ate
The endings: -i, -este, -eu, -emos, -eram.
Eu bebi um copo de vinho.
I drank a glass of wine.
Tu vendeste o carro?
Did you sell the car?
Ele recebeu a carta ontem.
He received the letter yesterday.
Nós aprendemos muito no curso.
We learned a lot on the course.
PRETÉRITO PERFEITO SIMPLES — VERBOS REGULARES
131

===== PAGE 132 =====

Elas correram cinco quilómetros.
They ran five kilometres.
DICA · TIP
For -er verbs the nós form is the same in the present and in the past: comemos can
be "we eat" or "we ate". Only the context — or a word like ontem — tells you which.
This is normal and native speakers live with it happily.
4. -IR verbs: partir
Remove -ir. The stem of partir is part-.
Pronome partir English
eu parti I left
tu partiste you left
ele/ela/você partiu he/she left, you left
nós partimos we left
eles/elas/vocês partiram they left, you left
The endings: -i, -iste, -iu, -imos, -iram.
Eu abri a janela da sala.
I opened the living-room window.
Tu decidiste bem.
You decided well.
O comboio partiu às oito em ponto.
The train left at eight o'clock sharp.
Nós dormimos oito horas.
We slept eight hours.
Eles assistiram ao jogo no estádio.
They watched the match at the stadium.
Here are the three sets of endings side by side. Learn this small grid and you know the
whole regular past tense.
PRETÉRITO PERFEITO SIMPLES — VERBOS REGULARES
132

===== PAGE 133 =====

Pronome -AR -ER -IR
eu -ei -i -i
tu -aste -este -iste
ele/ela/você -ou -eu -iu
nós -ámos -emos -imos
eles/elas/vocês -aram -eram -iram
5. Spelling changes in the eu form
Some -ar verbs change their spelling in the eu form only. Nothing about the verb is
irregular — the sound stays exactly the same, and the spelling has to change to keep it that
way, because the ending -ei begins with an e.
Infinitivo ends in eu form Why English
-car → ficar fiquei c → qu to keep the hard "k" sound I stayed
-çar → começar comecei ç → c, because c before e already sounds "s" I started
-gar → chegar cheguei g → gu to keep the hard "g" sound I arrived
Only the eu form changes. Everything else is completely regular:
Pronome ficar começar chegar
eu fiquei comecei cheguei
tu ficaste começaste chegaste
ele/ela/você ficou começou chegou
nós ficámos começámos chegámos
eles/elas/vocês ficaram começaram chegaram
More verbs of the same three families:
PRETÉRITO PERFEITO SIMPLES — VERBOS REGULARES
133

===== PAGE 134 =====

Verbo eu (PPS) English
ficar fiquei to stay, to be located
tocar toquei to touch, to play (music)
explicar expliquei to explain
brincar brinquei to play (like a child)
começar comecei to begin
almoçar almocei to have lunch
dançar dancei to dance
chegar cheguei to arrive
pagar paguei to pay
jogar joguei to play (a game)
entregar entreguei to hand in, to deliver
Ontem cheguei a casa às dez da noite.
Yesterday I got home at ten at night.
Fiquei em casa todo o fim de semana.
I stayed at home all weekend.
Comecei a trabalhar aqui em janeiro.
I started working here in January.
Paguei a conta com cartão.
I paid the bill by card.
ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
Do not write "ficei", "começei" or "chegei". Those spellings would change the
pronunciation to "fissei", "comesssei", "cheJei". The written change protects the
sound. And remember it happens only in the eu form: never "ficquaste" or
"cheguaste".
6. Time markers of the past
These words tell the listener you are in the past. Put them at the beginning or the end of the
sentence.
PRETÉRITO PERFEITO SIMPLES — VERBOS REGULARES

Português English

anteontem the day before yesterday

no ano passado last year
no sábado passado last Saturday

em 2019 in 2019

depois / a seguir afterwards / next
primeiro… depois… finalmente first… then… finally

Ontem à noite não dormi nada.

DICA · TIP
Há + a period of time means "ago": há dois dias = two days ago. It is the same há
you learned in Lição 07 for "there is / there are". Do not confuse it with the
preposition a.
NOTA CULTURAL
When a Portuguese person asks Então, que tal o fim de semana? they expect a short
story in the PPS: Fui ao Alentejo, comi bem, dormi pouco. Keeping the answer short
and modest is normal; long enthusiastic accounts can sound like boasting.

LIÇÃO 17 · UNIDADE A2
Pretérito Perfeito Simples — verbos irregulares
The simple past tense — irregular verbs
In Lição 16 you learned the regular past. Unfortunately, the verbs you use most often are
the irregular ones. In this lesson you get the complete set: ser/ir, ter, estar, fazer, poder,
dizer, ver, vir, dar, saber, querer, trazer, pôr and haver. They look frightening as a list,
but they fall into four families. Learn the families and you learn the verbs. After this lesson
you can tell any story about the past.
1. How irregular past verbs work
A regular verb keeps its stem and adds regular endings. An irregular verb in the PPS does
two things at once: it changes its stem, and it takes a slightly different set of endings.
Most irregular verbs share this one set of endings. We will call it the irregular pattern:
Pronome Ending Example: ter (stem tiv-)
eu -e tive
tu -este tiveste
ele/ela/você -e teve
nós -emos tivemos
eles/elas/vocês -eram tiveram
Notice the most useful fact in this lesson: in the irregular pattern the eu form and the ele
form are almost the same, and neither has an accent. This is the opposite of the regular
verbs (falei / falou).
Ontem eu não tive tempo.
Yesterday I didn't have time.
Ele teve um problema no trabalho.
He had a problem at work.
Nós tivemos uma reunião longa.
We had a long meeting.
DICA · TIP
Do not look for a rule that explains why the stems change. There isn't a useful one.
These fourteen verbs are the whole problem — once you know them, the rest of the
language is regular. Read the tables out loud; the rhythm does more than the analysis.
PRETÉRITO PERFEITO SIMPLES — VERBOS IRREGULARES
140

===== PAGE 141 =====

2. Family A — the -IV- group: ter and estar
Two verbs, one stem shape. The stem is tiv- and estiv-, with an e instead of an i in the ele
form.
Pronome ter English
eu tive I had
tu tiveste you had
ele/ela/você teve he/she had, you had
nós tivemos we had
eles/elas/vocês tiveram they had, you had
Pronome estar English
eu estive I was (temporarily)
tu estiveste you were
ele/ela/você esteve he/she was, you were
nós estivemos we were
eles/elas/vocês estiveram they were, you were
Eu estive em Lisboa durante três dias.
I was in Lisbon for three days.
Vocês estiveram na festa?
Were you at the party?
Nós tivemos de sair mais cedo.
We had to leave earlier.
Tu tiveste sorte.
You were lucky.
ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
Estive means "I was" only for a limited, finished period: Estive doente uma semana.
For a lasting state in the past you need the imperfeito (Lição 18) or fui. Also: never
write "estivi" or "tivi" — the eu ending here is -e, not -i.
3. Family B — the U group: poder, saber, pôr, haver
These four build their stem with a u: pud-, soub-, pus-, houv-. The endings are the same
irregular pattern.
PRETÉRITO PERFEITO SIMPLES — VERBOS IRREGULARES
141

===== PAGE 142 =====

Pronome poder saber pôr
eu pude soube pus
tu pudeste soubeste puseste
ele/ela/você pôde soube pôs
nós pudemos soubemos pusemos
eles/elas/vocês puderam souberam puseram
English translations: pude = I was able / I managed; soube = I found out / I knew; pus = I
put.
Não pude ir à reunião.
I couldn't go to the meeting.
Ele pôde sair mais cedo.
He was able to leave earlier.
Eu só soube a notícia ontem.
I only found out the news yesterday.
Onde é que tu puseste as chaves?
Where did you put the keys?
Ela pôs o livro em cima da mesa.
She put the book on the table.
DICA · TIP
Two circumflex accents matter here. pôde (he could, past) versus pode (he can,
present) — the accent is the only difference. And pôs (he put) versus pos- in other
words. Missing these accents changes the tense, so write them.
Haver is used almost only in the third person singular, meaning "there was / there were":
Pronome haver English
eu houve (rare)
tu houveste (rare)
ele/ela/você houve there was / there were
nós houvemos (rare)
eles/elas/vocês houveram (rare)
Houve um acidente na ponte.
There was an accident on the bridge.
Houve muitos problemas no início.
There were many problems at the start.
PRETÉRITO PERFEITO SIMPLES — VERBOS IRREGULARES
142

===== PAGE 143 =====

ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
Impersonal houve never becomes plural. Say Houve muitas pessoas na rua, not 
"Houveram muitas pessoas". Portuguese people make this mistake too, but in writing
it is considered wrong.
4. Family C — the hissing group: fazer, dizer, querer, trazer
These four have stems ending in a hissing sound: fiz-, diss-, quis-, troux-. Note that in this
family the ele form has no ending at all — the stem stands alone.
Pronome fazer dizer querer trazer
eu fiz disse quis trouxe
tu fizeste disseste quiseste trouxeste
ele/ela/você fez disse quis trouxe
nós fizemos dissemos quisemos trouxemos
eles/elas/vocês fizeram disseram quiseram trouxeram
English: fiz = I did/made; disse = I said; quis = I wanted; trouxe = I brought.
O que é que tu fizeste no fim de semana?
What did you do at the weekend?
Ele não fez nada o dia inteiro.
He did nothing all day.
Eu já lhe disse a verdade.
I already told him the truth.
Eles quiseram pagar a conta.
They wanted to pay the bill.
A Maria trouxe um bolo.
Maria brought a cake.
DICA · TIP
The x in trouxe is pronounced like an English "s" — "trôsse". Portuguese x has four
sounds; here it is the simplest one.
5. Family D — the short ones: ser/ir, dar, ver, vir
These do not follow the irregular pattern. They are short, very common, and must simply be
memorised.
PRETÉRITO PERFEITO SIMPLES — VERBOS IRREGULARES
143

===== PAGE 144 =====

Ser and ir share exactly the same forms in the past. Only the context tells you which verb it
is.
Pronome ser / ir English (ser) English (ir)
eu fui I was I went
tu foste you were you went
ele/ela/você foi he/she was he/she went
nós fomos we were we went
eles/elas/vocês foram they were they went
Ontem fui ao cinema.
Yesterday I went to the cinema.
O jantar foi muito bom.
The dinner was very good.
Eles foram a Espanha de carro.
They went to Spain by car.
DICA · TIP
If a preposition of movement follows (a, para, até), it is ir. If an adjective or a noun
follows, it is ser. Fui a Braga = I went to Braga. Fui professor = I was a teacher.
Pronome dar ver vir
eu dei vi vim
tu deste viste vieste
ele/ela/você deu viu veio
nós demos vimos viemos
eles/elas/vocês deram viram vieram
Eu dei o livro à minha irmã.
I gave the book to my sister.
Nós vimos um filme espanhol.
We saw a Spanish film.
Ela veio de comboio.
She came by train.
Vocês vieram a pé?
Did you come on foot?
PRETÉRITO PERFEITO SIMPLES — VERBOS IRREGULARES
144

===== PAGE 145 =====

ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
Three lookalikes to keep apart. vimos = "we see" and also "we saw" (verb ver). 
viemos = "we came" (verb vir). vimos is never the past of vir. And vim (I came) is not
vi (I saw).
6. The whole set on one page
Use this as your revision sheet. Cover the right-hand columns and test yourself.
Infinitivo eu tu ele/ela/você nós eles/elas/vocês
ser / ir fui foste foi fomos foram
ter tive tiveste teve tivemos tiveram
estar estive estiveste esteve estivemos estiveram
fazer fiz fizeste fez fizemos fizeram
poder pude pudeste pôde pudemos puderam
dizer disse disseste disse dissemos disseram
ver vi viste viu vimos viram
vir vim vieste veio viemos vieram
dar dei deste deu demos deram
saber soube soubeste soube soubemos souberam
querer quis quiseste quis quisemos quiseram
trazer trouxe trouxeste trouxe trouxemos trouxeram
pôr pus puseste pôs pusemos puseram
haver houve houveste houve houvemos houveram
NOTA CULTURAL
Two of these verbs carry a special past meaning. Soube is not "I knew" but "I found
out" — Soube ontem = I heard about it yesterday. And não quis is stronger than
English "didn't want": it means "refused". Ele não quis ajudar = he refused to help.
Use it carefully.
Vocabulário
a notícia the news item
a verdade the truth
a mentira the lie
o acidente the accident

LIÇÃO 18 · UNIDADE A2
Pretérito Imperfeito
The imperfect tense — "used to", habits and descriptions in the past
The second past tense. Where the Pretérito Perfeito Simples closes a box ("I ate at eight"),
the Pretérito Imperfeito leaves it open: what you used to do, what things were like, what
was going on around an event. It is easy to build — only four verbs are irregular (ser, ter,
vir, pôr) — and it is what you need to talk about your childhood, describe a place, and be
polite in a shop.
1. What the imperfeito is for
Four jobs. Learn them as four jobs, not as one vague feeling.
Job Example English
Repeated habit in the past Todos os dias ia a pé. Every day I used to walk.
Description / background A casa era grande e tinha um jardim. The house was big and had a garden.
Age, time, weather in the past Eram três da tarde e chovia. It was three in the afternoon and it was raining.
Action in progress (interrupted) Jantava quando o telefone tocou. I was having dinner when the phone rang.
Quando eu era pequeno, vivia no campo.
When I was little, I lived in the countryside.
Nós jogávamos à bola todas as tardes.
We used to play football every afternoon.
O quarto era pequeno, mas tinha muita luz.
The room was small, but it had a lot of light.
DICA · TIP
English gives you three translations for one Portuguese form. Eu comia can be "I
used to eat", "I would eat" (habit, not condition) or "I was eating". If any of those three
fits your English sentence, the imperfeito is the right tense.
2. Regular formation
-AR verbs take -ava; -ER and -IR verbs share -ia. That is the whole rule.
PRETÉRITO IMPERFEITO
150

===== PAGE 151 =====

Pronome falar English
eu falava I used to speak
tu falavas you used to speak
ele/ela/você falava he/she used to speak
nós falávamos we used to speak
eles/elas/vocês falavam they used to speak
Pronome comer partir English
eu comia partia I used to eat / leave
tu comias partias you used to…
ele/ela/você comia partia he/she used to…
nós comíamos partíamos we used to…
eles/elas/vocês comiam partiam they used to…
The endings alone:
Pronome -AR -ER / -IR
eu -ava -ia
tu -avas -ias
ele/ela/você -ava -ia
nós -ávamos -íamos
eles/elas/vocês -avam -iam
Antigamente eu trabalhava ao sábado.
In the past I used to work on Saturdays.
Tu bebias muito café.
You used to drink a lot of coffee.
Nós íamos à praia todos os verões.
We went to the beach every summer.
Eles abriam a loja às oito.
They used to open the shop at eight.
ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
The eu form and the ele form are identical: eu falava / ele falava. Portuguese usually
drops the subject pronoun, but in this tense you often have to keep it, or nobody
knows who you mean. Say Eu trabalhava em Lisboa, not just Trabalhava em Lisboa,
if the context is not clear.
PRETÉRITO IMPERFEITO
151

===== PAGE 152 =====

DICA · TIP
The nós form always carries an accent: -ávamos and -íamos. Never write 
"falavamos" or "comiamos". The accent marks where the stress falls, three syllables
from the end.
3. The four irregular verbs
Only four verbs in the whole language are irregular in the imperfeito: ser, ter, vir, pôr.
Three of them (ter, vir, pôr) build a stem in -nh-, so they are really one small pattern plus 
ser.
Pronome ser English
eu era I was
tu eras you were
ele/ela/você era he/she was, you were
nós éramos we were
eles/elas/vocês eram they were, you were
Pronome ter vir pôr
eu tinha vinha punha
tu tinhas vinhas punhas
ele/ela/você tinha vinha punha
nós tínhamos vínhamos púnhamos
eles/elas/vocês tinham vinham punham
English: tinha = I had; vinha = I used to come; punha = I used to put.
A minha avó tinha uma casa no Alentejo.
My grandmother had a house in the Alentejo.
Ele vinha cá todos os domingos.
He used to come here every Sunday.
Nós púnhamos a mesa antes do jantar.
We used to set the table before dinner.
Eu era muito tímido em criança.
I was very shy as a child.
PRETÉRITO IMPERFEITO
152

===== PAGE 153 =====

ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
Ir is not irregular in this tense: ia, ias, ia, íamos, iam. Learners often invent "inha"
by analogy with tinha. And note vinha (was coming) versus vinho (wine) — one letter,
very different dinner.
4. Costumava — the explicit "used to"
If you want to underline that something was a habit and is no longer, use costumar in the
imperfeito + infinitive. It is very common in speech.
Pronome costumar English
eu costumava I used to
tu costumavas you used to
ele/ela/você costumava he/she used to
nós costumávamos we used to
eles/elas/vocês costumavam they used to
Costumávamos passar o verão em Sesimbra.
We used to spend the summer in Sesimbra.
Ela costumava levantar-se às seis.
She used to get up at six.
Eu não costumava beber café.
I didn't use to drink coffee.
5. Polite imperfeito
In shops, cafés and offices the imperfeito replaces the present to make a request softer. It is
the Portuguese equivalent of English "I'd like" instead of "I want".
Direct Polite English
Quero um café. Queria um café. I'd like a coffee.
Pode ajudar-me? Podia ajudar-me? Could you help me?
Importa-se? Importava-se? Would you mind?
Gosto de ver. Gostava de ver. I'd like to see.
Queria uma bica e um pastel de nata, se faz favor.
I'd like an espresso and a custard tart, please.
Podia repetir, se faz favor?
Could you repeat that, please?
PRETÉRITO IMPERFEITO
153

===== PAGE 154 =====

Gostava de marcar uma consulta.
I'd like to make an appointment.
NOTA CULTURAL
In Portugal queria is the normal way to order. Quero um café sounds blunt, almost
rude, to a waiter. Add se faz favor (or por favor) and you sound completely natural. In
a café, uma bica in Lisbon and um cimbalino in Porto both mean a small black
espresso; um café works everywhere.
6. Useful time expressions
These words signal "repeated / ongoing" and pull the imperfeito towards you.
Português English
antigamente in the old days, formerly
dantes back then
naquela altura at that time
quando era pequeno/a when I was little
em criança as a child
todos os dias / todas as semanas every day / every week
sempre que whenever
normalmente / geralmente usually / generally
às vezes sometimes
de vez em quando from time to time
aos domingos on Sundays
durante anos for years
Antigamente não havia telemóveis.
In the old days there were no mobile phones.
Aos domingos almoçávamos em casa dos meus avós.
On Sundays we had lunch at my grandparents' house.
De vez em quando ela vinha de bicicleta.
From time to time she came by bicycle.
PRETÉRITO IMPERFEITO
154

===== PAGE 155 =====

DICA · TIP
Havia is the imperfeito of haver: "there was / there were" as a description. Havia
muita gente na praça = there were a lot of people in the square. Like há and houve, it
never goes plural.

LIÇÃO 19 · UNIDADE A2
Perfeito ou Imperfeito?
Choosing between the two past tenses — narrative and description
This is the hardest choice in A2, and the one that most marks you out as a confident
speaker. You already know both tenses. Now you learn when to use each: the perfeito
moves the story forward, the imperfeito paints the scene it happens in. You will get a
decision table, matched pairs of sentences that differ only in the tense, the structures 
costumava and estava a + infinitivo, and a full narrative to read.
1. The core idea: camera and photograph
Imagine you are filming. The imperfeito is the wide shot that establishes the scene — the
weather, the room, what people were doing, how they felt. The perfeito is each thing that
then happens, one after another.
Era domingo e chovia. Eu estava em casa e lia um livro.
It was Sunday and it was raining. I was at home and I was reading a book.
De repente, o telefone tocou. Levantei-me e atendi.
Suddenly the phone rang. I got up and answered.
The first sentence has no events — nothing moves. The second is pure event: three things
happen in order. That is the whole distinction.
DICA · TIP
Ask yourself one question: did something happen, or was something simply the case?
Something happened → perfeito. Something was the case → imperfeito. Most
sentences answer instantly.
2. The decision table
When you are unsure, run down this table. It is the practical version of the rule.
PERFEITO OU IMPERFEITO?
159

===== PAGE 160 =====

Question If yes → tense Example
Did it happen once, at a moment? Perfeito Ontem vi a Maria.
Did it happen repeatedly, as a habit? Imperfeito Todos os dias via a Maria.
Is it a description (place, person, weather,
feeling)?
Imperfeito Ela era alta e tinha olhos
verdes.
Is it an action with a stated start or end? Perfeito Trabalhei lá de 2010 a
2015.
Is it an action in progress when something
else happened?
Imperfeito (for the background), perfeito (for
the interruption)
Dormia quando ele chegou.
Is it the time, the date, or someone's age? Imperfeito Eram oito horas. Ela tinha
vinte anos.
Does the sentence say how many times, or
how long in total?
Perfeito Fui lá três vezes.
Is it a plan or intention in the past ("was
going to")?
Imperfeito of ir + infinitive Ia telefonar-te.
ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
English past simple is not a reliable guide. "I lived in Porto" is vivi no Porto if you
mean a finished period ("for three years, then I left"), but vivia no Porto if you are
describing the background of a story ("at that time I lived in Porto, and one day…").
The English is identical; the Portuguese is not.
3. Same sentence, two tenses
These pairs differ in one word. Read each pair aloud and feel the change of meaning.
Perfeito Imperfeito
Comi às oito. I ate at eight (and finished). Comia às oito. I used to eat at eight.
Ontem fui ao ginásio. Yesterday I went to the gym. Dantes ia ao ginásio. I used to go to the gym.
Soube a notícia. I found out the news. Sabia a notícia. I knew the news (already).
Ele quis ajudar. He tried / insisted on helping. Ele queria ajudar. He wanted to help.
Ela não quis vir. She refused to come. Ela não queria vir. She didn't feel like coming.
Pude entrar. I managed to get in. Podia entrar. I was allowed to get in.
Tive um filho. I had a child (event: birth). Tinha um filho. I had one child (state).
Conheci o Pedro em 2019. I met Pedro in 2019. Conhecia o Pedro. I knew Pedro.
Esteve doente uma semana. He was ill for a week (finished). Estava doente. He was ill (at that moment).
PERFEITO OU IMPERFEITO?
160

===== PAGE 161 =====

DICA · TIP
Four verbs change their English translation depending on the tense: saber (knew /
found out), conhecer (knew / met), querer (wanted / tried, refused), poder (was
able / managed). Memorise these four pairs — they appear constantly.
4. Background plus event in one sentence
The most common shape in storytelling: imperfeito … quando … perfeito. The imperfeito
is what was already going on; the perfeito breaks into it.
Eu jantava quando ela telefonou.
I was having dinner when she rang.
Chovia muito quando saímos de casa.
It was raining hard when we left the house.
Ele dormia quando o alarme disparou.
He was asleep when the alarm went off.
Estávamos à espera do autocarro quando começou a nevar.
We were waiting for the bus when it started to snow.
Two actions in the imperfeito together mean they were both going on at the same time; use 
enquanto.
Enquanto eu cozinhava, ele punha a mesa.
While I was cooking, he was setting the table.
Enquanto a avó contava histórias, nós ouvíamos em silêncio.
While grandma told stories, we listened in silence.
5. Estava a + infinitivo
In Lição 15 you learned estar a + infinitivo for "to be doing" in the present. Put estar in
the imperfeito and you get "was doing". It means almost the same as a plain imperfeito, but
insists more on the moment.
Pronome estar a + infinitivo English
eu estava a trabalhar I was working
tu estavas a trabalhar you were working
ele/ela/você estava a trabalhar he/she was working
nós estávamos a trabalhar we were working
eles/elas/vocês estavam a trabalhar they were working
Eu estava a ler quando ouvi o barulho.
I was reading when I heard the noise.
PERFEITO OU IMPERFEITO?
161

===== PAGE 162 =====

Eles estavam a discutir outra vez.
They were arguing again.
O que é que tu estavas a fazer às oito?
What were you doing at eight?
ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
Use this form only for something in progress at a point in time. For a habit you must
use the plain imperfeito: Antigamente trabalhava aos sábados, never "estava a
trabalhar aos sábados". And in Portugal it is a + infinitivo, not the Brazilian estava
trabalhando.
6. Costumava versus the plain imperfeito
Both express past habit. Costumava + infinitivo simply makes the habit explicit and
usually implies it has stopped.
Eu corria todas as manhãs.
I ran every morning.
Eu costumava correr todas as manhãs.
I used to run every morning (not any more).
Nós costumávamos passar o Natal no Porto.
We used to spend Christmas in Porto.
You cannot use costumava for a single event. Costumava ir lá ontem is impossible; say Fui
lá ontem.
7. Words that pull one way or the other
Pushes you to the PERFEITO Pushes you to the IMPERFEITO
ontem, anteontem antigamente, dantes
na semana passada, no ano passado naquela altura, em criança
de repente, nesse momento normalmente, geralmente
uma vez, duas vezes todos os dias, aos domingos
logo a seguir, finalmente sempre, nunca (as a habit)
em 2015, às oito horas (event) enquanto, sempre que
de… a… (from… to…) durante (in a description)
De repente, alguém bateu à porta.
Suddenly, someone knocked at the door.
Naquela altura ainda não havia internet.
At that time there was still no internet.
PERFEITO OU IMPERFEITO?
162

===== PAGE 163 =====

Duas vezes ele tentou e duas vezes falhou.
Twice he tried and twice he failed.
NOTA CULTURAL
Listen to how Portuguese people tell an anecdote in a café. It almost always opens in
the imperfeito — Eu estava na paragem, não estava ninguém… — and then switches to
the perfeito for the punchline: …e de repente apareceu um senhor com uma cabra.
Copying that rhythm makes your Portuguese sound native long before your vocabulary
does.

LIÇÃO 20 · UNIDADE A2
Pronomes complemento
Object pronouns — direct, indirect, placement and combined forms
Instead of repeating o livro, à Ana, aos meus pais in every sentence, Portuguese replaces
them with short pronouns. In this lesson you learn the direct set (o, a, os, as), the indirect
set (me, te, lhe, nos, lhes), the special shapes -lo/-la and -no/-na, the rules that decide
whether the pronoun goes after or before the verb, and the combined forms mo, to, lho,
no-lo. This is the most "Portuguese-looking" grammar in the book — and once it clicks, your
sentences shorten by half.
1. Direct object pronouns
The direct object is the thing the verb acts on directly, with no preposition: Eu vejo o filme.
Replace it with a pronoun that agrees in gender and number.
Pronome Forma English
eu me me
tu te you
ele / você (m.) o him, it, you
ela / você (f.) a her, it, you
nós nos us
eles / vocês (m.) os them, you
elas / vocês (f.) as them, you
Vejo o filme. → Vejo-o.
I see the film. → I see it.
Conheço a Ana. → Conheço-a.
I know Ana. → I know her.
Comprei os bilhetes. → Comprei-os.
I bought the tickets. → I bought them.
Ele ajuda-me sempre.
He always helps me.
ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
Do not use the subject pronoun as an object. "Eu vejo ele" is Brazilian colloquial
speech, not European Portuguese. Say Vejo-o. The same for "com ele", which is
correct after a preposition — the ban applies only to the direct object position.
PRONOMES COMPLEMENTO
167

===== PAGE 168 =====

2. Indirect object pronouns
The indirect object is the person something is given, said or sent to — in Portuguese,
introduced by a. Here gender does not matter; only number and person do.
Pronome Forma English
eu me to me
tu te to you
ele/ela/você lhe to him, to her, to you
nós nos to us
eles/elas/vocês lhes to them, to you
Dei o livro à Ana. → Dei-lhe o livro.
I gave the book to Ana. → I gave her the book.
Ele telefonou-me ontem.
He phoned me yesterday.
Escrevi-lhes um e-mail.
I wrote them an e-mail.
Podes emprestar-nos o carro?
Can you lend us the car?
DICA · TIP
Some verbs that take a direct object in English take an indirect one in Portuguese: 
telefonar a, responder a, perguntar a, agradecer a, pedir a, dizer a. So it is 
Telefonei-lhe ("I phoned him"), never "Telefonei-o".
3. Ênclise: the pronoun after the verb
The default position in European Portuguese is after the verb, joined with a hyphen. This is
called ênclise. If nothing in the sentence forces the pronoun forward, it stays behind.
Ele deu-me a chave.
He gave me the key.
Nós vimo-los no jardim.
We saw them in the garden.
Encontrei-a na estação.
I met her at the station.
PRONOMES COMPLEMENTO
168

===== PAGE 169 =====

ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
In Brazil the pronoun usually comes first: me deu a chave. In Portugal that is wrong at
the start of a sentence. Never begin a sentence with an object pronoun: Deu-me a
chave, not "Me deu a chave".
4. Próclise: the words that pull the pronoun forward
Certain words attract the pronoun to a position before the verb, with no hyphen. This is 
próclise. Learn the trigger list; it is finite.
Trigger type Words Example
Negatives não, nunca, nada, ninguém, nem Não o vi.
Question words quem, que, o que, onde, quando, como, porque,
quanto
Onde a puseste?
Subordinating conjunctions que, porque, se, quando, embora, enquanto, como Disse que me ajudava.
Certain adverbs já, ainda, sempre, também, só, apenas, talvez, bem,
mal, aqui, ali, cá, lá, pouco
Já te disse.
Indefinite / quantity subjects alguém, todos, tudo, muitos, poucos, alguns, cada um Alguém nos viu.
Prepositional phrases before
the verb
em, a, de + expression Em Lisboa encontrei-o / Antes
de lhe falar…
Não te ouvi bem.
I didn't hear you properly.
Ninguém me avisou.
Nobody warned me.
Quando o viste?
When did you see him?
Ela disse que nos telefonava hoje.
She said she would phone us today.
Também lhes escrevi.
I wrote to them as well.
Talvez a encontre amanhã.
Maybe I'll meet her tomorrow.
Compare the same sentence with and without the trigger:
PRONOMES COMPLEMENTO
169

===== PAGE 170 =====

Ênclise (no trigger) Próclise (trigger)
Vi-o ontem. Não o vi ontem.
Disse-lhe tudo. Já lhe disse tudo.
Ajudaram-nos muito. Sempre nos ajudaram muito.
Encontrei-a na rua. Onde é que a encontraste?
DICA · TIP
With estar a + infinitivo and ir + infinitivo you have a choice: the pronoun may
attach to the infinitive (Estou a vê-lo, Vou dizer-lhe) or, if there is a trigger, move
before the first verb (Não o estou a ver, Não lhe vou dizer). Both are correct.
5. The -LO / -LA forms: after -r, -s, -z
When the verb form ends in -r, -s or -z, that final letter disappears and the pronoun o / a /
os / as becomes -lo / -la / -los / -las. If the verb then ends in a stressed vowel, it takes an
accent.
Verb ends in Before After English
-ar comprar + o comprá-lo to buy it
-er fazer + o fazê-lo to do it
-ir abrir + a abri-la to open it
-ôr pôr + os pô-los to put them
-s (tu) fazes + o faze-lo you do it
-s (nós) fizemos + o fizemo-lo we did it
-z fez + o fê-lo he did it
-z diz + a di-la he says it
The accent rule is simple: -ar gives á, -er and -ez give ê, -ir and -iz give plain i with no
accent, -ôr keeps its ô.
Queres o livro? Vou comprá-lo amanhã.
Do you want the book? I'm going to buy it tomorrow.
É difícil, mas tens de fazê-lo.
It's difficult, but you have to do it.
A janela está fechada. Podes abri-la?
The window is closed. Can you open it?
Nós convidámo-los para o jantar.
We invited them to dinner.
PRONOMES COMPLEMENTO
170

===== PAGE 171 =====

Ele fê-lo sem ajuda.
He did it without help.
ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
Learners write "comprar-o", "fazer-o", "fizemos-o". These are impossible. The final -r /
-s / -z always drops: comprá-lo, fazê-lo, fizemo-lo. Note there is no accent on 
fizemo-lo — the stress is already where it should be.
6. The -NO / -NA forms: after a nasal ending
When the verb form ends in a nasal sound — written -m, -ão or -õe — the pronoun becomes
-no / -na / -nos / -nas. Nothing is dropped this time; the n is simply added.
Verb ends in Before After English
-m compram + o compram-no they buy it
-m fizeram + a fizeram-na they did it
-m tem + os tem-nos he has them
-ão dão + a dão-na they give it
-ão põe → põem + o põem-no they put it
-õe põe + a põe-na he puts it
Eles vendem-no na loja da esquina.
They sell it in the corner shop.
As crianças fizeram-na sozinhas.
The children did it on their own.
Ela põe-nas na gaveta.
She puts them in the drawer.
Os meus pais levam-nos à escola.
My parents take them to school.
DICA · TIP
This rule applies only to o, a, os, as. The indirect pronouns never change: deram-lhe,
dizem-me, põem-lhes — no -no, no -lo. Only the direct third-person forms have these
two disguises.
7. Combined forms: two pronouns at once
When both an indirect and a direct pronoun appear, they merge into a single word. Indirect
always comes first.
PRONOMES COMPLEMENTO
171

===== PAGE 172 =====

Indirect + o a os as
me mo ma mos mas
te to ta tos tas
lhe lho lha lhos lhas
nos no-lo no-la no-los no-las
vos vo-lo vo-la vo-los vo-las
lhes lho lha lhos lhas
Ele deu o livro a mim. → Ele deu-mo.
He gave it to me.
Eu dei a chave ao João. → Eu dei-lha.
I gave it to him.
Eles emprestaram-no-lo por uma semana.
They lent it to us for a week.
Quem to disse?
Who told you that?
Não lhos mostrei.
I didn't show them to him.
Note that lho covers both "to him/her" and "to them" — only the context tells you which.
And notice that nos + o keeps a hyphen: no-lo, not "nolo".
ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
Do not confuse the combined mas (= me + as, "them to me") with the conjunction 
mas ("but"), or no-lo with the contraction no (em + o). Position and context make it
clear, but they trip readers up. In everyday speech many Portuguese speakers avoid
the combined forms and say Ele deu-me o livro instead — you should still recognise
them in writing.
NOTA CULTURAL
Pronoun placement is the clearest grammatical border between European and
Brazilian Portuguese. A Portuguese speaker writes Ajudou-me muito; a Brazilian writes
Me ajudou muito. Neither is wrong in its own country, but in Portugal — in a job
application, an exam or an e-mail — ênclise is expected.
Vocabulário
o complemento the object (grammar) a frase the sentence

Exercício 6 — Leitura: Um favor entre vizinhos
Read the text and answer the questions in Portuguese, in full sentences. Hard words: a escada = ladder, stairs; a estante =

Na semana passada o meu vizinho bateu à porta. Precisava de uma escada e

Ele agradeceu-me muito e levou-a. No dia seguinte, quis montar uma estante, mas
não tinha chave de fendas. Telefonou-me e eu emprestei-lhe também a minha.
Passaram três dias e ninguém me devolveu nada. Como não o queria incomodar, não
lhe disse nada. Ao sábado, ele apareceu com a escada, a chave de fendas e um bolo
que a mulher fez. Deu-mo à porta e disse: "Isto é para si." Comi-o ao lanche e achei
que valeu a pena esperar.

Quantos dias passaram sem devolver nada?

O que é que o vizinho trouxe ao sábado?

LIÇÃO 21 · UNIDADE A2
Futuro e Condicional
The future and the conditional
In this lesson you learn the two tenses that talk about what will happen and what would
happen. Both are built the same way — you add endings to the whole infinitive, not to a
stem — so learning one gives you the other almost free. You will also meet the only three
irregular verbs (fazer, dizer, trazer), see the very Portuguese habit of using the future to
guess rather than to predict, learn the polite queria / gostaria / podia formulas, and —
most useful of all in daily life — practise the simple spoken alternative ir + infinitivo.
1. The Futuro Simples: how it is built
This is the easiest tense in Portuguese. You do not remove anything from the verb. Take the
complete infinitive — falar, comer, partir — and add one small set of endings. The endings
are the same for all three verb families.
Pronome Ending English
eu -ei I will…
tu -ás you will…
ele/ela/você -á he/she will…, you will…
nós -emos we will…
eles/elas/vocês -ão they will…, you will…
Now the same endings on three real verbs. Notice that the infinitive is still visible inside
every form.
Pronome falar comer partir English (falar)
eu falarei comerei partirei I will speak
tu falarás comerás partirás you will speak
ele/ela/você falará comerá partirá he/she will speak
nós falaremos comeremos partiremos we will speak
eles/elas/vocês falarão comerão partirão they will speak
Amanhã eu falarei com o diretor.
Tomorrow I will speak to the director.
O curso começará em setembro.
The course will begin in September.
FUTURO E CONDICIONAL
177

===== PAGE 178 =====

Nós partiremos às seis da manhã.
We will leave at six in the morning.
Eles comerão no hotel.
They will eat at the hotel.
Tu verás que não é difícil.
You will see that it is not difficult.
DICA · TIP
Every future form except nós carries a written accent: -ás, -á, -ão. The eu form -ei
and the nós form -emos have none. If you write falara without the accent you have
written a completely different (and very literary) tense, so the accent is not
decoration.
2. The Condicional: how it is built
Exactly the same idea. Take the full infinitive and add -ia, -ias, -ia, -íamos, -iam. In
English this is "would".
Pronome falar comer partir English (falar)
eu falaria comeria partiria I would speak
tu falarias comerias partirias you would speak
ele/ela/você falaria comeria partiria he/she would speak
nós falaríamos comeríamos partiríamos we would speak
eles/elas/vocês falariam comeriam partiriam they would speak
The conditional endings are the same letters as the endings of the imperfeito of -er/-ir
verbs, which you already know from Lição 18. The difference is what they are attached to:
the imperfeito attaches them to a stem (com-ia), the conditional attaches them to the whole
infinitive (comer-ia).
Eu gostaria de falar com a Dra. Sousa.
I would like to speak to Dr. Sousa.
Nós preferiríamos um quarto com vista.
We would prefer a room with a view.
Ela ficaria contente com esta notícia.
She would be happy with this news.
Sem ti, eles não conseguiriam nada.
Without you, they would not manage anything.
Tu venderias a tua casa por esse preço?
Would you sell your house for that price?
FUTURO E CONDICIONAL
178

===== PAGE 179 =====

ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
Watch the nós accent: falaríamos, comeríamos, partiríamos — always an accent on
the í. Learners regularly write falariamos, which is simply wrong. The same accent
appears in the imperfeito (comíamos), so it is one habit, not two.
3. The three irregular stems: far-, dir-, trar-
Only three verbs in the whole language refuse to use the full infinitive. They shorten it, and
then take completely normal endings. Learn these three shapes and you have finished the
irregulars of both tenses.
Infinitivo Stem Futuro (eu) Condicional (eu) English
fazer far- farei faria to do, to make
dizer dir- direi diria to say, to tell
trazer trar- trarei traria to bring
Full tables. Compounds behave the same way: desfazer → desfarei, refazer → refarei, 
contradizer → contradirei.
Pronome fazer (futuro) dizer (futuro) trazer (futuro)
eu farei direi trarei
tu farás dirás trarás
ele/ela/você fará dirá trará
nós faremos diremos traremos
eles/elas/vocês farão dirão trarão
Pronome fazer (condicional) dizer (condicional) trazer (condicional)
eu faria diria traria
tu farias dirias trarias
ele/ela/você faria diria traria
nós faríamos diríamos traríamos
eles/elas/vocês fariam diriam trariam
Eu farei o possível para chegar a horas.
I will do my best to arrive on time.
O que é que tu dirias ao teu chefe?
What would you say to your boss?
FUTURO E CONDICIONAL
179

===== PAGE 180 =====

Eles trarão a sobremesa.
They will bring the dessert.
Nós faríamos a mesma coisa no teu lugar.
We would do the same thing in your place.
DICA · TIP
Verbs like ter, ser, ir, pôr, ver and vir are wildly irregular in other tenses but
perfectly regular here: terei, serei, irei, porei, verei, virei; teria, seria, iria, poria,
veria, viria. Note that pôr loses its circumflex once an ending is added: porei, not 
pôrei.
4. What these tenses actually mean
The future tense does two jobs in Portuguese. The first is the obvious one, and it is the rarer
one in speech.
(a) Prediction and commitment. Announcements, forecasts, plans, promises, written
notices.
A loja abrirá no dia 3 de maio.
The shop will open on 3 May.
Amanhã choverá em todo o país.
Tomorrow it will rain across the whole country.
Voltarei em breve, prometo.
I will come back soon, I promise.
(b) Probability — guessing about the present. This use is very common and surprises
English speakers. The future tense does not talk about the future at all here; it means
"probably", "must be", "I wonder".
Que horas serão?
I wonder what time it is.
Ele estará doente, não veio trabalhar.
He must be ill — he didn't come to work.
Ela terá uns quarenta anos.
She must be about forty.
Onde estarão as minhas chaves?
Where on earth can my keys be?
The conditional also does two jobs.
(a) Politeness. This is the reason most learners need the conditional at all. It softens a
request so that it does not sound like an order.
FUTURO E CONDICIONAL
180

===== PAGE 181 =====

Direct Polite English
Quero um café. Queria um café. I would like a coffee.
Quero ver o quarto. Gostaria de ver o quarto. I would like to see the room.
Pode ajudar-me? Podia ajudar-me? Could you help me?
Importa-se de esperar? Importar-se-ia de esperar? Would you mind waiting?
Deve descansar. Devia descansar. You should rest.
DICA · TIP
In real speech the Portuguese very often use the imperfeito instead of the conditional
for politeness: queria, podia, importava-se, gostava. In a café, Queria um galão, se
faz favor is the natural order; Quereria would sound strange. Use gostaria and 
gostava freely — both are correct, gostava is warmer and more spoken.
(b) Hypothesis. What would happen in an imagined situation. You will meet the full "se +
imperfeito do conjuntivo" pattern in Lição 29; for now just recognise it.
Com mais tempo, eu aprenderia a tocar piano.
With more time, I would learn to play the piano.
No teu lugar, eu não diria nada.
In your place, I wouldn't say anything.
Ninguém faria isso de propósito.
Nobody would do that on purpose.
5. Pronouns in the middle: mesóclise (recognition only)
In Lição 20 you learned that an object pronoun normally goes after the verb with a hyphen: 
dá-me, viu-o. With the future and the conditional something odd happens in careful written
Portuguese: the pronoun goes inside the verb, between the infinitive and the ending. This is
called mesóclise.
Verb + pronoun Becomes English
dar + lhe (futuro) dar-lhe-ei I will give him/her
dizer + lhe (futuro) dir-lhe-ei I will tell him/her
fazer + o (futuro) fá-lo-ei I will do it
dar + lhe (condicional) dar-lhe-ia I would give him/her
ajudar + te (condicional) ajudar-te-ia I would help you
Dar-lhe-ei uma resposta na segunda-feira.
I will give him an answer on Monday.
FUTURO E CONDICIONAL
181

===== PAGE 182 =====

Com todo o gosto, ajudar-te-ia.
I would gladly help you.
You need to recognise this, not produce it. Two escape routes are always available and
always correct:
Mesóclise (formal) Everyday alternative English
Dar-lhe-ei o livro. Vou dar-lhe o livro. I'll give him the book.
Dir-te-ei tudo. Eu digo-te tudo. I'll tell you everything.
Ajudar-te-ia. Eu ajudava-te. I'd help you.
ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
You may not simply write "lhe darei" to avoid the problem. In European Portuguese a
pronoun does not start a clause. If something already pulls the pronoun forward — a
negative, a question word, words like já, também, só, talvez — then próclise applies
normally and the problem disappears: Não lhe direi nada. · Quando lhe dirás?
6. The everyday future: ir + infinitivo
Here is the honest truth about spoken European Portuguese: people rarely use the futuro
simples for real future plans. They use ir in the present + the infinitive, exactly like English
"I'm going to". You met this in Lição 10; now compare it directly with the tense you have just
learned.
Pronome ir (presente) + infinitivo English
eu vou vou estudar I'm going to study
tu vais vais estudar you're going to study
ele/ela/você vai vai estudar he/she is going to study
nós vamos vamos estudar we're going to study
eles/elas/vocês vão vão estudar they're going to study
Futuro simples (written, formal) ir + infinitivo (spoken) English
Falarei com ele amanhã. Vou falar com ele amanhã. I'll speak to him tomorrow.
Faremos o jantar. Vamos fazer o jantar. We'll make dinner.
Eles chegarão tarde. Eles vão chegar tarde. They'll arrive late.
Trarei o carro. Vou trazer o carro. I'll bring the car.
The plain present tense is also used for the near future when a time word makes things
clear — again just like English.
FUTURO E CONDICIONAL
182

===== PAGE 183 =====

Amanhã trabalho em casa.
Tomorrow I'm working from home.
O comboio parte às seis.
The train leaves at six.
NOTA CULTURAL
Portuguese speakers often soften a promise about the future rather than commit hard
to it. Depois vemos ("we'll see later"), logo se vê ("it'll sort itself out") and se calhar
("maybe") are everywhere. The futuro simples, by contrast, sounds official — you will
meet it most in the news, on signs, in contracts and in weather forecasts rather than in
a conversation over coffee.
Vocabulário

a previsão the forecast, the prediction

o conselho the (piece of) advice

Exercício 6 — Leitura: A entrevista da Marta
Read the text and answer the questions in Portuguese, in full sentences. Hard words: a entrevista = the interview; a sede = the

A Marta terá uma entrevista na próxima quinta-feira, numa empresa com sede no

LIÇÃO 22 · UNIDADE A2
O Imperativo
The imperative — giving instructions, orders and advice
This lesson teaches you how to tell someone to do something — and how to tell them not to.
You will learn the affirmative and negative imperative for tu, você and vocês, the simple
rule that derives almost every form from the present tense you already know, the handful of
irregular verbs, where object pronouns go, and how the imperative really appears in
Portuguese life: in recipes, on street signs, in instructions, in directions and in friendly
advice.
1. Three people, four boxes
Portuguese has no single "imperative". It has a small grid. You need four boxes: tu
affirmative, tu negative, você (both), vocês (both). The good news is that three of the four
come from the same place.
Pessoa Afirmativo Negativo English
tu fala não fales speak / don't speak (friend)
você fale não fale speak / don't speak (polite)
vocês falem não falem speak / don't speak (plural)
Look at the grid again. Only the tu affirmative box (fala) is different from the rest.
Everything else — não fales, fale, não fale, falem, não falem — is one and the same
family of forms. Learn that family once and you have three quarters of the imperative.
Fala mais devagar, por favor.
Speak more slowly, please. (to a friend)
Fale com a minha colega.
Speak to my colleague. (polite)
Não falem todos ao mesmo tempo.
Don't all speak at the same time.
2. Where the forms come from
Rule 1 — the tu affirmative. Take the ele/ela form of the present tense and use it exactly
as it is. Nothing is added, nothing is removed.
O IMPERATIVO
187

===== PAGE 188 =====

Infinitivo ele/ela (presente) tu (imperativo) English
falar fala fala! speak!
comer come come! eat!
abrir abre abre! open!
fechar fecha fecha! close!
beber bebe bebe! drink!
dormir dorme dorme! sleep!
Rule 2 — everything else. Take the eu form of the present tense, remove the final -o, and
swap the vowel: -ar verbs take -e, and -er/-ir verbs take -a. Add -s for tu negative, -m for 
vocês.
Infinitivo eu (presente) Stem você vocês não + tu
falar falo fal- fale falem não fales
comer como com- coma comam não comas
abrir abro abr- abra abram não abras
fazer faço faç- faça façam não faças
dizer digo dig- diga digam não digas
pôr ponho ponh- ponha ponham não ponhas
vir venho venh- venha venham não venhas
ouvir ouço ouç- ouça ouçam não ouças
pedir peço peç- peça peçam não peças
sair saio sai- saia saiam não saias
This is a powerful rule, because it means every present-tense irregularity in the eu form is
carried straight over. If you can say eu faço, you can say faça, façam and não faças
without learning anything new.
Faça o favor de entrar.
Please come in. (very polite formula)
Ponham os casacos ali.
Put your coats over there.
Não saias sem guarda-chuva.
Don't go out without an umbrella.
Peça ajuda ao segurança.
Ask the security guard for help.
O IMPERATIVO
188

===== PAGE 189 =====

DICA · TIP
The spelling changes you met in Lição 16 come back here, for the same reason —
protecting the sound before a new vowel. ficar → fique, chegar → chegue, começar
→ comece, pagar → pague, almoçar → almoce. Never write fice or chege.
3. Full tables for the three verb families
Pronome falar (afirm.) falar (neg.) English
tu fala não fales speak / don't speak
você fale não fale speak / don't speak
vocês falem não falem speak / don't speak
Pronome comer (afirm.) comer (neg.) English
tu come não comas eat / don't eat
você coma não coma eat / don't eat
vocês comam não comam eat / don't eat
Pronome abrir (afirm.) abrir (neg.) English
tu abre não abras open / don't open
você abra não abra open / don't open
vocês abram não abram open / don't open
ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
The commonest learner error is saying "não fala!" to a friend. That is the affirmative
form with a não glued in front, and it is wrong. The negative tu imperative always
ends in -s: não fales, não comas, não abras, não te preocupes. Say the affirmative
and the negative together when you learn a verb — fala / não fales — and the pair will
stick.
4. Irregular imperatives
Only a few verbs break the rules, and almost all of them break only the tu affirmative box.
Here is the complete working list.
O IMPERATIVO
189

===== PAGE 190 =====

Infinitivo tu (afirm.) tu (neg.) você vocês English
ser sê não sejas seja sejam be
estar está não estejas esteja estejam be (state)
ir vai não vás vá vão go
ter tem não tenhas tenha tenham have
fazer faz não faças faça façam do, make
dizer diz não digas diga digam say, tell
dar dá não dês dê deem give
ver vê não vejas veja vejam see, look
vir vem não venhas venha venham come
pôr põe não ponhas ponha ponham put
saber sabe não saibas saiba saibam know
querer quer não queiras queira queiram want
ler lê não leias leia leiam read
trazer traz não tragas traga tragam bring
Sê paciente, ele já vem.
Be patient, he's coming.
Vá sempre em frente e depois vire à direita.
Go straight ahead and then turn right.
Tem cuidado com o degrau!
Be careful with the step!
Não tenha pressa, temos tempo.
Don't rush, we have time.
Diga, se faz favor.
Yes? / How can I help you? (what a shop assistant says)
DICA · TIP
Three of these are useful whole phrases. Diga! is what someone says when they pick
up the phone or serve you at a counter — it means "go ahead, I'm listening", not
literally "say". Faça favor (or Se faz favor) is the standard "please" and also "excuse
me" to get attention. Olhe ("look") is used constantly to open a sentence, roughly like
English "look, …".
O IMPERATIVO
190

===== PAGE 191 =====

5. Where the pronoun goes
You learned in Lição 20 that an object pronoun normally follows the verb with a hyphen, but
jumps in front after a negative. The imperative follows exactly that rule, and it is the
clearest place to see it.
Afirmativo — pronoun after Negativo — pronoun before English
Diz-me a verdade. Não me digas nada. Tell me the truth. / Don't tell me anything.
Dê-me um quilo, por favor. Não me dê tanto. Give me a kilo. / Don't give me so much.
Senta-te aqui. Não te sentes aí. Sit here. / Don't sit there.
Levante-se devagar. Não se levante ainda. Get up slowly. / Don't get up yet.
Chamem-nos amanhã. Não nos chamem tão cedo. Call us tomorrow. / Don't call us so early.
Ajuda-me com esta mala, se faz favor.
Help me with this suitcase, please.
Não te preocupes, eu trato disso.
Don't worry, I'll deal with it.
Sirva-se, está à vontade.
Help yourself, make yourself at home.
ERRO COMUM · COMMON MISTAKE
When the verb form ends in -r, -s or -z, the direct pronouns o, a, os, as become -lo, -
la, -los, -las and that final letter disappears: faz + o → fá-lo, diz + a → di-la, traz +
os → trá-los. These forms are rare in speech; most people simply repeat the noun — 
Faz o trabalho. That is perfectly good Portuguese.
6. The imperative in real life
Recipes. Portuguese recipes are usually written in the você form, or with the infinitive
alone, which is even more impersonal.
Descasque as batatas e corte-as em rodelas.
Peel the potatoes and cut them into slices.
Junte o azeite e deixe cozinhar durante vinte minutos.
Add the olive oil and let it cook for twenty minutes.
Bata os ovos e tempere com sal e pimenta.
Beat the eggs and season with salt and pepper.
Signs and notices. Public signs use você forms, or the infinitive, or the impersonal não é
permitido.
O IMPERATIVO
191

===== PAGE 192 =====

Sinal English
Empurre / Puxe Push / Pull
Não fume Do not smoke
Mantenha a porta fechada Keep the door closed
Aguarde a sua vez Wait your turn
Não pise a relva Keep off the grass
Tire a senha Take a ticket (queue number)
Desligue o telemóvel Switch off your mobile
Em caso de incêndio, use as escadas In case of fire, use the stairs
Directions. Almost always você, because you are talking to a stranger.
Siga em frente até ao semáforo.
Carry straight on as far as the traffic light.
Vire à esquerda na segunda rua.
Turn left at the second street.
Atravesse a praça e desça a rua.
Cross the square and go down the street.
Não vá por ali, está em obras.
Don't go that way, there are roadworks.
Softening an order. A bare imperative can sound harsh. Portuguese softens it constantly.
Softener Example English
se faz favor / por favor Feche a porta, se faz favor. Close the door, please.
faça o favor de + infinitivo Faça o favor de esperar. Kindly wait.
importa-se de + infinitivo Importa-se de repetir? Would you mind repeating?
pode + infinitivo Pode assinar aqui? Can you sign here?
vamos + infinitivo Vamos começar. Let's begin.
lá (friendly particle) Anda lá! Come on!
NOTA CULTURAL
The vamos form fills the gap where English has "let's": Vamos embora (let's go), 
Vamos a isto (let's get on with it). And note how much work the tiny word lá does in
friendly commands — Diz lá, Vá lá, Espera lá. It has nothing to do with "there"; it
simply takes the edge off the order, the way English adds "then" or "go on".
O IMPERATIVO
192

===== PAGE 193 =====

Vocabulário
a receita the recipe; the prescription
o ingrediente the ingredient
a colher the spoon, the spoonful
o forno the oven
a panela the saucepan
o lume the heat, the flame (on the hob)
